Owed by the council

#CounterpartyAttached toOwed byWOriginStatus

Empty at tick 0. It will not stay that way.

F-005 is the live demonstration of the attachment rule. It is the heaviest favour on the board and the only one that can be erased by a single death.

ledger/favours.md
ledger

Hooks

Things that can be used, or threatened to be used. A hook is not spent by using it - blackmail costs standing, and the hook remains.

State

  • quiet - the mark does not know the hook exists, and therefore cannot defend against it.
  • known - the mark knows. Using a hook flips it, and the flip is one-way.

Holding quietly and using are different choices. The second cannot be undone.

Underpinning

The axis is deniability, not strength. A hook with nothing behind it is waved away. Evidence is not required, but it is what turns an accusation into a thing that cannot be dismissed. Testimony can be discredited; a document in the mark's own hand cannot.

Potency

A number, 1-10, on the same principle as favour weight - what the mark will spend to make it go away.

The mark's counter-move

Once a hook is known, the mark works to defuse it between ticks, whether or not the council thinks about it. Releasing the secret on their own terms. Framing another. Stealing or destroying the evidence. Discrediting the witness. Becoming too useful to burn.

Every known hook is therefore a live enemy agenda, and is run as one.

Held by the council

#MarkAttached toStatePUnderpinningCounter-move

Empty at tick 0. The five have been in the city a matter of days.

Held against the council

#MarkAttached toStatePUnderpinningCounter-move

Empty at tick 0, and it stays empty until the council does something worth hiding. Exposure is earned by their own conduct and is never handed to them.

The Watch

The council's only armed body. Mechanics in ../rules/mass-combat.md.

Two hundred and fifty men, and the reason the charter means anything.

Strength and pay

Establishment (the council's number)250
Present strength250
Wage10G per man per week
Weekly payroll2,500G (the cost base carries 2,800G, including sergeants and gate officers)
Arrears5 weeks - 12,500G, on a published schedule with a date against each
Paid against arrears so far7,500G - payments 1, 2 and 3 of 8, ticks 5, 6 and 7, each in the week it was dated

The council sets both numbers. It may raise the wage, or set the establishment to whatever it can afford. It pays for every man on the roll, every week, for as long as he is on it.

At full strength nobody is recruited. Recruiting fills the gap between present strength and establishment and does nothing else, so expanding the Watch is a decision with a bill attached rather than something that happens on its own.

Condition

Standing-9 (see factions.md)
Cohesion ceiling60 + standing = 51
Current cohesion51 - at the ceiling
BandWAVERING (50-74)
Below half strength?no

**The Watch has closed a tick out of breaking for the first time since the charter**, by exactly one point, and it did not happen because anything was done to the Watch.

It happened because the council told somebody else no. Four days after refusing the Watch a promise of notice before a search, it refused the harbour guard the same thing, in person, on the same stated ground. +2, for consistency in public. Roper's whole comment on it was "They told the guard no as well", and then, after a while, "Same clause. Same week."

The arithmetic: standing -12 to -10, ceiling 48 to 50, and cohesion recovers at +10 a tick toward the ceiling, so 48 goes to the ceiling and stops. It has taken no losses, so cohesion sits at its recovery ceiling and tracks standing point for point. The two cannot move in opposite directions while the company is intact.

Tick 7 added one more. Payment 3 of 8, handed over in the muster yard by the councillor who commands them, in the week it was dated. Three for three. Garrow did not make a speech about it and Roper did not ask for one.

Two points of band is still not a margin. Any single search, audit or broken date costs three to six points of standing, and three points puts them back in breaking with the ceiling and the cohesion falling together on the same day.

And they watched another collector get audited this week. The gate side of the toll was searched on the Monday and was clean, for the sixth week running, and nothing was posted about it because nothing was found on their books and the council's undertaking attaches to a finding. The quay side was searched on the Friday and was not clean. Roper has said nothing about that at all, which Garrow noted in the minute and did not interpret.

Cohesion, tick by tick

TickClosing standingCeilingCohesionBand
0-204040breaking
2-134747breaking
3-194141breaking
4-194141breaking
5-124848breaking
6-105050wavering
7-95151WAVERING

**Seven ticks, two of them out of breaking, and both were bought by things that happened to somebody else.** Tick 6 came from refusing the harbour guard; tick 7 came from a payment the council had already promised. Nobody has spent an action on the Watch's condition in either week.

A chase cannot rout them. Cohesion spent chasing is tiredness, not a battle: 2 a beat, floored at 25, no hold check. What it buys the far side is the following week, when a company that chased on Tuesday has to fight on Friday. See ../rules/chase.md.

What breaking costs, today:

Robbery response30% + standing = 21% - still slow to wade in
Robbery outcome0 on the situation score - the band penalty is gone, at 50 exactly
Mass combatthe hold check no longer bites after every lost exchange

The standing lands twice, for two different reasons, exactly as robbery.md says it should - and the second of the two stopped landing this week, which is the first mechanical dividend the Watch's standing has ever paid.

Strength, tick by tick

TickOpeningRecruitedLostClosing
4250--250
5250--250
6250--250
7250--250

The Watch is at establishment, so nothing is being recruited and nothing is being spent on recruiting.

If it takes losses - a robbery gone badly, a rout - it refills at the rate its standing allows:

At the present -9 standingabout 4 men a week
At 05 a week
At +2010 a week

So fifty men lost today would take three months to replace. The same fifty would take ten weeks if the Watch thought well of the council.

Desertion is not modelled. Parked by the DM - men do not currently leave over unpaid wages, which is generous and known to be generous. It is also the only reason the second search cost standing and not headcount.

Equipment

Issuebillhook, jack, helm. Gate officers carry swords
Missilenone worth counting
Horseeleven, for messages and the upper road

A Watch, not a garrison. Equipped to hold a gate and walk a ward, and to do nothing else at all.

No deployment in tick 6, and one was considered and declined. Garrow took a pilot and a boat to the upper boundary stone instead, to establish what the Watch could and could not have been asked to do on the water: "Past the stone the Watch doesn't go. I'm not putting that to them and they're not asking me to." The chase was not mounted and no cohesion was spent on it. See ../transcripts/tick-006-a-hundred-and-eight.md.

Threads

What the council has to pull on. Mechanics in ../rules/investigations.md.

Freshness, not strength. The council sees a word, never a number: fresh, fading, cold, dead. A thread that is fading may have been neglected or may have been tampered with. There is no way to tell by looking.

Open threads

As at the end of tick 7.

#ThreadRests onFreshnessWhose leadWhat it might give
T-010 (tick 7, from T-008)The suretyworking record (the Hithe porterage list) + a living manfreshThackerayCrispin Vout, who wrote his name in the margin against an account that is not a business - and who was paid by somebody to do it
T-004 (tick 2, from T-002)The muster cycleworking record (the council's own copy)fadingKeelwhose calendar it is, and what it is counting
T-009 (tick 6, from T-006)The man with the thumbarchived-grade working record (Odo Tench's day-book, eleven years, one hand)fadingBrackwatera date, an injury, a fee - and the name he gave
T-007 (tick 5, from T-006)The hire at the lower stairworking record (Rudd's hire book)coldBrackwatera hand that is not a name - if the book is ever opened
T-002 (tick 2, from T-003)The Galician factorarchived record (the bills themselves, by number)coldThackeraywho paid for the lot, and in what coin
T-008 (tick 5, from T-005)The accountcasual memorycoldPartridgeThurlow and Company, carriers. Largely spent - what it had, it has given

Four of six are cold, and the investigation has never been in better shape. That sentence is the whole of what the freshness words do and do not tell the council. Three of the four cold threads rest on paper, which does not rot and does not care what word is next to it on this page: T-002 is archived, T-007 is a book on a shelf at a stair, and T-009's day-book runs back eleven years.

What is actually rotting is the council's permission to ask. A pull costs ten points whether it succeeds or fails, and the council has now spent fifty points of T-002 on three attempts and found nothing twice.

"It is paper, it is archived, it does not rot, and it will be exactly where it is in a year. I went and asked the wrong questions of it for a day and a half." - Thackeray

#ThreadDiedWhat was got out of it first

T-006 (tick 3)The messengertick 6He was withdrawn, not run - and on the week it died, one of the two men who did it.

T-008 is the new angle on T-005. T-009 is the new angle on T-006. T-010 is the new angle on T-008. Three generations of thread off two dead ones, in three weeks.

What the council has, in plain terms

Each point carries the threads it came off and the tick it was learned. The Investigation board is drawn from these tags and from the lineage in the tables above; the tags are stripped before the page is rendered.

  1. The jewellery was dwarven vault-work, nine pieces, sold eleven months ago by a river crewman called Hallet, who vanished inside the month. He paid his score at the Blackcap in full on his last night, which is not what a man does who expects to be back.
  2. Hallet was no thief. He was hired by the week for lifting, by a man who paid coin and drank with nobody, and he went upriver, north bank, about a day's pull. Two men who hired out at the same bench are still in the city.
  3. The lot was bought under a Galician factor's mark with no family name on it, paid in bills of exchange. Thackeray's reading: for a Galician that is not discretion, it is dishonour. A man trading behind a bare mark is a man whose name has been taken off him, and there will be a decision about it in a Galician court.
  4. That mark is paid on an eleven-week cycle that is a military pay cycle, not a trade one. Somebody is paying the way an army pays a standing post.
  5. Somebody amended those entries the week after the council read them. A correction that nobody asked for, filed by the mark's Needleford correspondent on instruction. The man behind the mark is not in this city, has a man in it, and takes about a fortnight to be frightened.
  6. A man came up a back stair and bought the auctioneer's memory. Brackwater had his face, his lodging and his hours.
  7. He did not run. He was withdrawn - a room emptied between midnight and first light by two men who were not him, and a boat hired before dawn, upriver.
  8. The lifting was hired on an account and not by a man: Thurlow and Company, carriers. There is no Thurlow and Company in the carting guild's list of yards, in the dues roll, in the waggon licence book or in the bond register. And Hallet was told the work would run through the winter.
  9. One of the two who emptied the room came back for a bag in the morning, after light, and stepped round a child on the stair and said sorry. Middling height, a good coat, bad boots, and a right thumb that does not bend.
  10. The thumb is a crush at the base, set fast and badly by a man in a hurry, and Odo Tench set it in the autumn before last. It is in the day-book, with a date and a fee and a second dressing the man never came back for. The name he gave was Thurlow.
  11. The account exists in exactly one place in Needleford: the Hithe porterage list, which records accounts that are good for wages rather than businesses. Opened the autumn before last, eleven entries, settled every Friday, never queried. And there is a column for who stood for it, with Crispin Vout in it - a bill-broker who vouches accounts for a fee and asks nothing. Eight of his other nine are spotless.
  12. Everybody the council asks about stops being in Needleford. The auctioneer was bought. The messenger was taken out between midnight and first light. The man who hired the boat has not been at the stair since the week the council came, and a boat went up on a Tuesday night with two aboard and one came back. Three, in five weeks.

Points ten and eleven are the same man. A man with a crushed thumb walked into a barber-surgeon's shop in the autumn before last and gave the name Thurlow; in the same autumn an account called Thurlow and Company, carriers opened on the porterage list and began hiring river men by the week off a bench on Fetter Lane, and Hallet was one of them.

"We have been looking for a business. There isn't one. There is a man with a stiff thumb who calls himself Thurlow, and he is in this city, and he was in that lane three weeks ago carrying a bag in daylight."

Point twelve is the first thing the council has that is about them rather than about itself. Brackwater's reading: "They cannot answer a question. They can only remove the person it was asked about. There are not very many people in a thing like this. They have been spending them."

Pulls that found nothing

The empty weeks are half the story. A pull costs the same whether or not it yields, rolls for notice the same, and still takes 10 off the thread. These are drawn on the Investigation board as cut strings - a string that starts at the thread and stops.

ThreadTickWhat was tried, and what came of it
T-0034Thackeray, the auctioneer off the Hithe. Fit +2 against 8, rolled 1. He had been bought and had forgotten. The burn paid out regardless and the thread died this tick.
T-0064Brackwater, the tail. Fit +3 against 7, rolled 3. Nothing - and it was noticed.
T-0065Brackwater, the lodging houses at the lane end. Fit +1 against 7, rolled 1. She can follow a man in this city and she cannot ask in it.
T-0076Brackwater, the earlier hires on the same business. Fit +3 against 7, rolled 2. She asked for the book. Rudd had given her a night because she asked for a night.
T-0026Thackeray, the archived bills by number. Fit +2 against 7, rolled 3. A day and a half of the wrong questions.
T-0066Brackwater, the emptied room. Fit +3 against 8, rolled 1. A limewashed room, a family of five, and a boot-nail in a floor joint.
T-0027Thackeray, the bills again. Fit +2 against 7, rolled 4 - failed by one. Second failure running on the one thread that cannot be waited out. Keel let it go without comment, which Thackeray noticed.
T-0077Brackwater, the stair, asked around with no names. Fit +1 against 7, rolled 2. Nothing on the question she went with - and the answer to a question she had not asked walked up to her anyway.

Not threads

  • The request to the Galician house. Sent tick 2, unanswered. Six ticks.
  • Harrowby staithe. A standing arrangement with the manor court, made this week, that the council hears what stops there and what does not. Not an interception and not a thread - a place that will have something to say the first time anything moves on that river. ../maps/the-river.md.
  • The porters' dispute at the Hithe. Heard, decided in part, and the forty of them have a longer memory than the ruling.
  • The missing 376G. Not looked at since tick 5.
  • The hundred and eight, twice. The guilds remitted 1,092G where 1,200G was assessed, in tick 6 and again in tick 7. Audited in tick 7 and the audit failed by one. The council found nothing, posted that it had looked, and paid six points of standing to the bloc that is mid-petition.
  • The three hundred and fifteen. Found, recovered, prosecuted and posted. Not a thread, because it is finished - which is the only item ever to leave this file by being solved.

Upkeep

Condition and arrears on the three cuttable lines. Mechanics in ../rules/upkeep.md.

Condition is not automatically visible. Dredging shows at 40 or below, quays and roads at 20 or below, relief and fire never. Above that it must be inspected - one action, which returns the true number, no roll. The event roll is never visible.

Condition

As at the end of tick 7.

LineWeeklyConditionBandKnown to the council?Funded this tick
Dredging700G60wornyes - inspected, tick 5yes - full
Quays, bridge, roads, lamps900G70wornyes - inspected, tick 2yes - full
Relief, fire, contingency400G80soundyes - inspected, tick 6yes - full

The quays, inspected - tick 2

Garrow spent an action walking the line and the council has the true number: 70, worn. The bridge is sound. The lower quays are not - two berths below the lime wharf are taking waggons they should not be taking, and the lamps east of the Hithe have not been relit since the winter before last.

Dredging, inspected - tick 5

Garrow went down the river with a pilot and a sounding line, with the one action he had left, and nobody had asked him to.

60, worn. The head of navigation is shoaling, slowly and exactly as it has been for years. Nothing is wrong this week. Nothing will look wrong until 40, and at 40 the pilots will already have been working around it for months.

Full funding holds both lines where they are. It does not improve them. To raise a line the council must overfund it, and the cost base has 2,500G a week of wage arrears on it until tick 12.

Relief, fire and contingency, inspected - tick 6

Garrow again, with the one action nobody asked him to spend.

**80, sound.** The cheapest line on the sheet and the healthiest. Two engines, both serviceable; the ward buckets counted and short in one ward only; the gaol fever money untouched since the plague-ship summer.

He said plainly in chamber why he had gone and looked at it: relief has the most room to fall and the fewest men watching it fall. A council in weekly deficit with six payments left on a published schedule will eventually have to stop paying for something, and this is the line it will reach for first - which is why Garrow wanted the number on paper before anyone proposed cutting it, and not after.

All three cuttable lines are now known by number, for the first time since the charter: quays 70, dredging 60, relief 80.

Full funding holds all three where they are. It does not improve them.

Arrears

Work the city is owed. Repaid at 2 + (consecutive ticks neglected / 4) times what was saved.

LineTicks neglectedSavedOwed in work
Dredging0--
Quays, bridge, roads, lamps0--
Relief, fire, contingency0--

Total arrears: none. Seven weeks, every line funded in full.

And the council is in weekly deficit anyway, for the third week running. The chest fell 206G in tick 7 - the smallest of the three falls, because 315G was recovered from a collector and 108G was not. The first week the council has to choose is the first week this table stops being empty, and the catch-up multiplier starts at 2.

The three numbers above are now the shape of that choice. 900G of quays at 70, 700G of dredging at 60, 400G of relief at 80. The cheapest line to stop paying is the one in the best condition and the one whose failure is the only one that can take condition off another line.

Windfalls

One-off opportunities, taken and missed. Mechanics in ../rules/windfalls.md.

rolled is standing-based and arrives at the start of a tick; earned comes off a good margin and arrives mid-tick; authored is the simulation adding one outside the rules, which it is not currently doing.

The log

TickSourceFactionWhat was offeredCost to takeTaken?Outcome
2rolledthe citizenryNan Shilling brought the burial club's almshouse lease and a week to answer it - an instrument that sold a reversion the club did not own.2 actionsyesSuccess, margin 1. The club kept the almshouse. Citizenry +4, sea factors -3.
3rolledthe citizenryForty porters at Vesk & Sons' lower berth, docked a third of a week, about to put an eleven-ton crane in the river.2 actions, 8 of the WatchyesSuccess, margin 0 - the barest. The crane is standing. Turned by an unpaid gate man, not by Partridge. Citizenry +3, sea factors +2. The dispute was postponed, not settled.
4--No roll hit (69.4 vs 34).---

6--No roll hit (95.5 vs 36.75). Not close.---
7--No roll hit (43.2 vs 36.75). Missed by six. Two actions were held in reserve for it.---

Standing rolls

pool x 1.0%, capped at 60%. Remote and institutional factions quartered.

TickPoolChanceResult
226.7527%hit (17.5) - citizenry
330.7531%hit (12.7) - citizenry
433.7534%miss (69.4)
535.7536%miss (38.0)
636.7537%miss (95.5)
736.7537%miss (43.2)

Two hits in six rolls at about 33%. Four misses running. Still well inside what six rolls can say, and noted rather than acted on. Noted in ../infra/calibration-log.md.

Two actions were held in reserve in tick 7, by Garrow and Partridge, for the first time since tick 4 - and the roll had already missed. The council spent everything for two weeks and caught nothing; it held two and caught nothing. Both outcomes are the mechanic working and neither is evidence about the other, which is worth writing down before somebody draws the wrong lesson from a sample of one. The rules are explicit that it will not always: a council that spends every councillor down in parallel can never catch a windfall.

Tick 8 - the pool as it now stands

FactionStandingInto the poolChance of being the source
The citizenry+3030.0077%
The Crown+307.50 (remote, quartered)21%
League of Kingdoms+51.25 (remote, quartered)3%
Pool38.75

Chance of a windfall in tick 8: 39% - the highest it has been. The citizenry gained two points for a council that posted an inconvenient finding and then acted on it, and the citizenry is three-quarters of the pool.

Everything else in the city is below zero, and four of them are live or arrive next week. The citizenry is three-quarters of the council's luck, and it is the one bloc that cannot pay them anything but errands.

  hard below an old barrow - a manor  |
                               court  |
                cover +2 - eyes none  |
                          [ 18h up ]  |
                                      |
                                      |  4h down  /  6h up   shallow, stony
                                      |
                                   ===o===  L-15  THRUSHFORD
                                      |     town, the ford - its own, and the
                                      |     Crown's road
                                      |     [ 24h up ]
                                      |
   upriver - the ford at Thrushford   |
                                      v

The river runs down the page. Hours are loaded travel from the council’s own quays, each in its own direction. The writ ends at the boundary stones.

The river Needleford is the ford of. Tidal to the city and no further: the city is the head of navigation, which is the whole reason it exists. Below the city a seagoing hull can come up on the tide. Above it, everything moves by pull, pole and horse, and moves slowly.

This map is a graph, not a picture. The landings are nodes, the reaches between them are edges, and the drawing at the top of this page is rendered from the two tables below. Edit the tables; the picture follows.

How to read the numbers

  • Cover is how easily a person moves unseen: scored -2 to +3, the same scale as everything else. It modifies the fit of anyone trying not to be followed, and the difficulty of anyone trying to follow.
  • Eyes is whether a stranger is remembered afterwards. High eyes is bad for the quarry and good for the investigator the week after. The two are not the same number and should not be collapsed into one.
  • Whose law decides whether the council may simply act. Past the boundary stones it may not, and that is a legal problem rather than a distance one.
  • Up and Down are hours of travel, loaded, in ordinary water. They differ because the current does. Below the city they also depend on the tide; a hull that misses it waits.

Landings

City node: L-06 - hours on the drawing are measured from here.

Painted blocks, for the look of the thing and for the one fact that matters: where a boat cannot be seen from the bank.

What this costs the council

Past the boundary stones the council has no writ, and the Watch outside the city is the legally challengeable act already settled in ../infra/open-questions.md. A pursuit upriver is therefore two problems: the boat, and the right to be in it.

There is no second crossing until Pike's landing. Anything put ashore on the north bank above the stones is, for the council, on the far side of a day.

A day's pull upriver, north bank, lands between Cold Elms and Barrow hard. That is three landings and the withies between them, and the withies are the only stretch on this river where a boat cannot be seen from the bank.

maps/the-river.md
rules

Character Sheets

Every named character in the simulation has one - councillor, clerk, harbour thief, envoy. If a new NPC is needed mid-play, they are given a sheet at the moment they are named.

Fields

FieldNotes
NamePlus any title or style they are addressed by
HealthOne value from the enum below
StrengthsUp to 7 tags
WeaknessesUp to 7 tags
Role-playUp to 7 tags. How they behave, not what they can do
VoiceHow they sound. Not adjectives - sentence length, what they never say, verbal habits, how they answer a question. Must be specific enough that a line of dialogue can be checked against it
DescriptionA few lines. Behaviour, manner, what they are like in a room

Voice is a separate field from Role-play on purpose. Role-play says how they behave in a room; Voice says how the sentences come out. Added by the DM after tick 5, because scene prose had drifted toward reporting what a character said instead of letting them say it, and "dry, formal" is not enough to write a line from.

Seven tags per category is a hard ceiling. A character who needs more tags than that is really two characters, or a badly understood one.

Health enum

healthy - injured - bloodied - dying - dead

Ordered by severity. A character does not skip downward without something having happened that is worth writing down.

Extending this

The schema will grow as the simulation does. New fields are added here first, then backfilled across existing sheets - never bolted onto one character privately. If a field only ever applies to one person, it belongs in their description.

rules/character-sheets.md
rules

The Chase

A pursuit of one or a few, across ground, where the question is distance.

Parked from the first week of design and built after tick 5, when play reached one and the simulation declined to improvise it. The thing it was waiting for was the map, which now exists: ../maps/the-river.md.

Not the rule for finding out where somebody went - that is a thread pull, see investigations.md. Not the rule for the fight at the end of it - that is party-combat.md, and a chase can turn into one without warning.

What a chase is, and what it is not

It is a stern chase. The quarry has a head start, measured in hours or minutes, and the question is not do I take him at this corner but can I close the gap before the trail, the ground or my authority runs out.

That is the shape play actually produced. Nobody in Needleford sprints down an alley: the council hires a boat, sends the Watch, or goes itself, and the thing resolves over hours. A foot chase through two wards is the same mechanic with a smaller clock.

Tracking is not chasing. Establishing that a man was put in a boat before dawn is a thread pull, and it has already been done that way. A chase begins only when the council knows a direction and moves on it. Tracking tells you the lead; the chase spends it.

The scale is declared, and the rules do not change

One beat is whatever the chase says it is - and it is declared before the first roll, exactly like a difficulty.

ChaseOne beatA lead of 6
Through the wards, on foota few minutesa couple of streets
The upper road, mountedhalf an hourthree hours
Up the Needle, by boatthree hourseighteen hours - tick 5's lead

Everything below is in beats. The lead is in beats, the reach is in beats, the cohesion bill is per beat. Nothing in the arithmetic knows or cares whether a beat is four minutes or three hours, which is the whole point: one rule, any scale.

The map measures in hours from the city node, so a river chase converts directly.

The order of play

Load-bearing, and the same as everywhere else in this ruleset.

  1. The council commits - who goes, by what means, under which stance, and whether the Watch goes at all.
  2. Then the beat unit, the lead, the reach, the fits and the quarry's difficulty are declared, in the resolution record.
  3. Then the beats are rolled.

Committing blind is what makes sending the wrong person a real mistake. A council told the gap is six beats before it decides who goes is a council that only ever sends Brackwater.

What it costs

Who goesActionsAnd
The Watch, or hired men1cohesion - see below
A councillor, in person2the health enum, and whatever the quarry has waiting
A councillor with the Watch2both

The Watch is not free. Somebody has to order it out, and that somebody is a councillor spending an action. A free option is an option that is always taken, and then the decision this mechanic exists to pose - do we risk her? - never gets made.

Two actions for going in person is the councillor premium, and it buys the better instrument: a councillor brings a real fit, an act, and the ability to decide something at the far end.

Means

Who goes sets the fit. What they go in sets a modifier. They are separate questions and a chase usually gets both wrong at once.

ByBeat modifierNote
On foot, against a boat or a horse-2the most common way a chase is lost before it starts
On foot, against a man on foot0
A hired boat, on the river0money, and an hour finding one willing
The Watch's horses - there are eleven+1the upper road only. Useless on the water
A Crown cutter, or the garrison's+2a favour, and the Crown asking what for

The beat

Both sides roll every beat and the rolls are compared - the same shape as a fight, deliberately, because a chase is the other thing that happens in minutes.

pursuer:  d10 + fit + means modifier
quarry:   d10 + fit + cover

The quarry's fit is scored against what the beat demands exactly as a councillor's is, off whatever the far side is. Cover comes off the map - the Cover column in ../maps/the-river.md, -2 to +3 - and it is why the Withies are worth running into and the weighing yard quays are not.

Pursuer winsthe lead closes
Quarry winsthe lead opens
Tiethe lead is unchanged, and a beat has gone

How far the lead moves, by the margin of the beat:

MarginBeats
0-21
3-52
6-83
9+4

The step is at 3 because that is where every other threshold in this ruleset sits. A chase therefore runs about as long as the lead is large, and a decisive beat is worth two ordinary ones rather than ending it.

Acts

Every named character in a chase gets exactly one act, on the same terms as a fight: one each, for the whole chase, and holding it for the beat that needs it is the decision.

An act in a chase turns one of three dials:

DialExample
DistanceBrackwater cutting across the osier beds while the boat takes the bend - the lead closes without a beat being won
Groundshutting a gate, waking a ferryman, putting the Watch across the only bridge - the quarry's cover drops for a beat
Reacha flare, a dog, a man sent up the bank to watch - the chase does not end in Lost this beat, whatever the number says

This is the reason to send more than one person. Two councillors on a chase is two acts, and acts are the only thing in the mechanic that beats the arithmetic.

The far side's named people have acts too.

How a chase ends

Four ways, and three of them are not failure.

Caught - the lead reaches 0

The quarry is taken, or is close enough that the next thing is a conversation or a fight.

Lost - the lead reaches the reach

reach = the starting lead + 3 - cover

Cover sets how far behind you can fall and still be following something. On the open Shelf at cover 0, a pursuer may drop five beats behind and still see a sail. In the Withies at cover +3, the reach equals the lead and the first beat the quarry wins ends it.

A lost chase is not a dead end. It mints a thread, and the freshness is set by the eyes of the ground it was lost on:

Eyes where it was lostThe thread
heavyfresh. Somebody saw, and somebody remembers
lightcold
nonenothing. There is no one to ask

This is the whole reason cover and eyes are two numbers. The ground that is best to lose a quarry in is the ground that leaves you nothing afterwards, and a council that chases into the Withies and loses him has lost him twice.

Out of bounds - the quarry passes the limit of the writ

The boundary stones are soft, and they are soft in different ways for different people.

  • The Watch will not cross. Not a thing the council overrules by saying so. Garrow will not order it and the rankers will not go, and a council that insists is spending standing with the only armed body it commands.
  • A councillor may cross. It is a decision, taken in the moment, and it is severity-scored - a Bryten officer operating outside his writ, with standing exposure to the Crown and to whoever's ground it is, and real canon-risk. It is allowed to halt the simulation.
  • Past the stones the chase continues with whoever crossed, at whatever they brought with them, and the means modifier does not improve.

The cheap option evaporates at exactly the point the chase gets interesting. That is the map's best feature and it is not an accident.

Ambush - the quarry stops running

The far side may spend its one action for the tick turning and fighting. That is not a new mechanic: it is the adversary action budget in investigations.md, spent on this instead of on a cover or on the plan.

The chase ends and party-combat.md begins, on the quarry's ground, with the pursuer's acts already spent if they were spent.

A chase is therefore never safe to mount, and the council cannot tell which chases are safe, because what the far side is spending its action on is sealed.

What a chase costs the people in it

The Watch - cohesion, and never a rout

2 cohesion per beat run

A chase cannot rout the Watch and cannot force a hold check. They are not under attack; they are tired. A chase may take a company down to the bottom of breaking - 25 - and no further.

It recovers at the normal +10 a tick toward the ceiling of 60 + standing, so a five-beat chase costs ten points and a fortnight of getting it back.

What it buys the far side is the week after. A company that chased on Tuesday is fragile on Friday, and at breaking every lost exchange in a real fight already forces a hold check. The cost of a chase is not paid during the chase.

A councillor - one level, at most

A councillor in person who loses a beat by a margin of 3 or more takes one level on the health enum - a fall on a stair, a boat hook, a lane that was a trap.

At most one level per chase. The standing rule is that no single event risks more than three levels; a chase is stricter than that on purpose, because a chase is not a fight. An ambush is a fight, and party combat's limits apply there instead.

A councillor who goes in already hurt can therefore be killed by a chase that turns. Going in wounded is the danger, exactly as everywhere else.

Stance - how a councillor travels

Declared at commit, with everything else. Two, and they are opposites.

Under colour of authorityUnmarked
Cooperationmay requisition, command, invoke the charter. +1 on any beat where a landing, a ferryman or a hire book must be made to helpnothing. Asks like anyone else, and pays
Coverthe councillor's own, and they are recognisable+2 cover, which is Brackwater's whole trade
Afterwardseyes apply. A record is left, and the far side learns who camenothing to find, and nothing to produce if stopped
Past the stonesthe offence is operating outside the writthe offence is whatever the locals decide it is

Only Brackwater is good at the second one, and the council knows it. Keel cannot go unmarked; he is the most recognisable man in Needleford and would be doing it badly.

Interception

Getting ahead instead of following, and it is the best play available.

The map supports exactly one honest chokepoint between the city and Thrushford, plus the stones themselves. A council that puts men on the right one does not need to be fast.

The gate is intelligence, not dice.

An interception may be laid only at a point a thread actually indicates.

Not a guess between two landings. Not a hunch. A thread - fresh, fading or cold

  • that says this is where he must pass, or an intel bonus taken as the ground.

This is deliberate, and it is the most important line in this file. A council that may guess a chokepoint and roll has a coin flip that skips the entire investigation game, and the investigation game is what this simulation is. Gated this way, interception is the pay-off for good tracking rather than an alternative to it.

Resolving one

A single opposed check, not a run of beats - the one-beat case.

the men laid there:  d10 + fit + position
the quarry:          d10 + fit + cover

Position is -2 to +3 for how well the point was chosen and how early they got there: a bridge with both ends held at dusk against a single landing watched from one bank in the rain.

Interceptor winscaught. No beats, no lead, no chase
Quarry winshe passes, and the lead opens by the margin - the men are in the wrong place and now behind
Tiehe is seen and not stopped. The thread is renewed and the chase may be mounted from here

Costs 1 action to lay, or 2 if a councillor is standing in it. The Watch pays 4 cohesion for a night of waiting, whether or not anyone comes.

What the council sees

The lead, in the units of the chase. The ground. Whether they are closing.

Not the quarry's fit, not the quarry's roll, and not whether the far side has an action left to spend on an ambush. They find that out the way they find out everything else.

Open

  • Chases in the city. The ward map does not exist yet. The mechanic is scale-free and will take one when it is drawn.
  • A quarry who is not a person - a cargo, a boat, a shipment moving on a schedule. Probably this rule with the fit replaced by a timetable, but untested.

Parked

  • Multi-party chases. Three sides on the water. Wanted by nobody yet.
rules/chase.md
rules

The Economy

Scope: three streams. Not a simulated market, not a cargo ledger. Three rates the council sets, each paid by a different bloc, each with a consequence that lands on a gauge.

Cargo-level detail exists, but only on demand - see Manifests below.

The three streams

StreamPaid bySqueezing it hurts
Passage tollEverything that changes hands - hulls inbound, waggons outboundThe traffic itself. Raise it and the throughput falls, and the city stops being worth courting.
Bonded-warehouse feesWhoever stores goods between sea and roadNobles and guild masters. They own the yards.
Guild and market duesTrades, stalls, licences, the working cityThe citizenry. These are paid by people who live here.

The toll is the large one and the dangerous one: it is simultaneously the council's chief revenue and its chief political instrument. The other two are smaller, and are chosen when the council wants someone specific to pay.

Each tick: revenue is throughput times rate, with traffic falling away as the toll climbs. The passage toll and bonded storage are then multiplied by the seasonal modifier in seasonality.md; guild and market dues are not. Rate changes write to ../ledger/factions.md like anything else.

The cost base

What the council pays for each week, at the city's ordinary size. Live figures are in ../state/treasury.md; this is the shape of it.

OutgoingWeeklyWhat it is
The Watch2,800G250 men at 10G, plus sergeants and gate officers
Customs establishment1,100GWeighers, tollkeepers, clerks - the cost of collecting the money
Chamber, courts and gaol600GThe charter grants adjudication; adjudication has staff
Quays, bridge, roads, lamps900GUpkeep of the things that make it a transfer point
Dredging700GAveraged. The head of navigation silts
Crown remittance1,500GThe charter's price, paid quarterly, reckoned weekly
Poor relief, fire, contingency400GThe first thing anyone proposes cutting
Total8,000G

These figures are post-legal-leakage. Everyone in the collection has been paid their due before the money reaches the chest. Theft and refusal are a separate matter - see leakage.md.

At the old rates the city took about 8,800G - a margin of 1.1, and nothing more. Needleford pays for itself and does not profit.

Dredging and poor relief are the dangerous lines. They are the only two that can be cut without anyone noticing within the week, and the only two whose neglect compounds. Cutting either should be cheap now and expensive later, by design.

That is now a mechanic - see upkeep.md, which tracks condition and arrears on all three cuttable lines.

How traffic answers the toll

Modelled on hysteresis in trade flows - the beachhead effect. Trade is quick to leave and slow to return, because a merchant who has rerouted has paid to establish agents, storage and contracts elsewhere and will not pay again merely because the old price came back.

Needleford is measured against Oldhithe and Thrushford, both worse, and neither worse by much. That gap is the council's whole margin.

1. The dead band

A change of 5% or less moves no traffic. Rerouting costs money and nobody pays it to save a trifle.

It is not free. A rate that creeps costs standing even when it costs no revenue - the affected bloc notices the pattern long before it notices the sum, and a council that raises the toll quietly every few ticks is a council that is seen to be doing exactly that. Small rises are cheap in gold and never cheap in goodwill.

2. Elasticity - where traffic ends up

A 10% rise in the toll costs about 15% of traffic, in the long run.

Past roughly a 25% rise the city collects less in total than before. There is a ceiling, it is lower than instinct suggests, and finding it by experiment is expensive.

3. Asymmetric speed - how fast it gets there

Traffic moves toward its new level at different rates in each direction:

DirectionPer tickMeaning
Falling50% of the gapA rise bites within a fortnight
Rising10% of the gapUndoing it takes most of a year

A toll rise that proves wrong is not corrected. It is served out.

4. Confidence

A council that changes the rate repeatedly teaches the factors that the rate is unreliable. Each change inside a short span narrows the dead band and slows the return further. Being wrong is one sin; fiddling is a different one.

Worked example

The council raises the toll 20%. Long-run traffic settles at -30%.

TickTrafficWhat the council sees
+1-15%Revenue up. The decision looks correct.
+2-22%Revenue flat. Unease.
+3-26%Revenue below where it started.
+4-28%They put the rate back.
+13-3%Roughly recovered.

Nine ticks of earning the old rate on reduced traffic, from one week's decision, with the worst of it arriving only after the surplus has been spent.

Upgrade path

This is deliberately a strict subset of a cargo-level economy. If the simulation ever wants consignment-level accounting as standing practice, the three streams stay exactly as they are and manifests are promoted from on-demand to continuous. Nothing here is thrown away by that change.

Manifests

The query is the mechanic

Forty hulls and three hundred waggons a week cannot be enumerated and will not be. Nothing is generated until someone asks a specific question.

The narrower the question, the more detail it returns. "What has been moving through the city?" returns volume and nothing else. "Grain coming upriver from Hallowmouth in the last fortnight, bonded, not yet collected" returns a manifest worth reading.

A vague question is not refused. It is answered vaguely, which is realistic and self-limiting.

Declared and actual

A manifest shows what was declared. It is not necessarily true.

  • Every generated manifest is written twice at the same moment: the declared version under simulation/, the actual under infra/sealed/manifests.md, tick-stamped.
  • The truth is fixed before the heroes look. What is in a barrel is never decided at the moment somebody opens it. If it were, the investigation would be theatre.
  • Misdeclaration is a legitimate and expected tool for the adversary, and for ordinary smugglers who have nothing to do with the mission.

One mistake per misdeclaration

Every misdeclared consignment carries exactly one pullable flaw, recorded with it under seal. A weight that does not match the bond value. An insurer who would never cover that route. A weighmaster's signature from a day he was ashore. A bond paid in the wrong coin.

Not more than one, because the thing is supposed to be hard. Not fewer than one, because otherwise reading the books is a coin flip, and Ambrose Keel's competence means nothing.

A search may return nothing

The adversary is not obliged to use the declared channel. If the answer is always where the heroes happen to look, the simulation is cheating on their behalf.

Absence is information. Cargo that should have been declared and was not is a finding, not a dead end.

What a search costs

Searching is never free. It costs on three axes at once:

  1. Standing. Bonded goods sit in the nobles' and guilds' yards, under their harbour guard, and belong to people with guildhall seats. A search is a political act. A search that finds nothing is a political mistake, and writes a negative entry against the nobles-and-guilds gauge.
  2. Favours. Searches that need cooperation - the harbour guard's books, a weighmaster's memory, a factor's private ledger - may require spending one, or incurring one.
  3. Time. See below. This is the real limiter.

Time, and the tick's attention

A tick is one week, and a councillor is one person.

Each councillor has 3 actions per tick. An action is anything that takes days and attention: pursuing a line of inquiry, a cargo search, a journey, a negotiation conducted properly, sitting with a dying witness.

  • An ordinary records search costs 1.
  • A physical search - opening bonded goods, walking a yard, stopping a hull - costs 2, because it needs permission first.
  • Governing is not free either. A crisis handled properly consumes actions that were going to be spent on the errand.

This is the mechanism that stops the council searching everything. They cannot investigate and govern in the same week without choosing which one suffers.

These numbers are provisional and are calibration targets. If the council is never short of time, the number is too high.

rules/economy.md
rules

Investigation Threads

A thread is a lead the council could pull: a name, a record, a mark on a quay, a man who saw something. Threads are the only way the council learns anything it was not told.

Every thread has a strength, a substrate, and someone on the other end.

Strength and freshness

Strength runs 0-100. It decays every tick whether or not anyone touches it.

The council never sees the number. It sees a word:

StrengthShown as
70-100fresh
30-69fading
1-29cold
0dead

A dead thread is not deleted. It yields nothing on a pull, and stays on the board as a reminder of what was not done. A new angle - a fresh witness, a second ledger - can revive it, but the original is gone.

Substrate

What the thread rests on sets how fast it rots. This table is open: when a thread rests on something not listed, a new row is reasoned from what it actually depends on and added here.

SubstrateDecay / tickWhy
Casual memory20a drunk at the weighing yard; a face in a crowd
Trained memory10the auctioneer's eye - he remembers pieces for a living
Physical trace15tides, traffic, a shed swept out
Working record2a ledger in daily use; wears by rebinding, loss and fire
Archived record0never decays on its own - but one cover burns it outright
Witness8, with a cliffa person can sail, be bought, or drown; may fall to 0 in one tick

The asymmetry is the point. Paper is permanent and fragile; memory is perishable and robust. Records cannot be waited out, so they must be destroyed, and destroying them is loud. Memories need only be outlasted.

A thread may rest on more than one substrate. It then decays at the slowest rate, because the slowest part is the part still standing - but a cover against the durable part collapses it to the fast one.

Pulling a thread

Intrusiveness buys information and spends secrecy on the same scale. No pull is both cheap and quiet. This table is open in the same way.

PullCostNoticed
Ask around, no names1 action10%
Ask by name1 action30%
Records query under authority1 action, standing with the holder40%
- naming a past weeksame cost, same noticeallowed
Surveillance1 action, resolves next tick20%
Physical search or seizure2 actions, standing, often a favour70%

A records query may name a week that has already gone. Ruled by the DM at halt 001: the books are the books, and a query that says the second week of the rate-book is no harder to serve than one that says this week. It costs the same, it is noticed the same, and it is the obvious answer to an audit that searched the wrong seven days.

A failed pull costs the same and rolls for notice the same. Asking badly is still asking.

Whether a pull was noticed is sealed. See ../infra/sealed/threads.md. The council is never told. They find out when the far side moves.

Who is pulling, and how well

There are no aptitude stats. At each pull the thread is weighed against the councillors actually doing the work, from their sheets in ../canon/the-council.md.

The order of play

Load-bearing, and not optional. The same ordering as a petition.

  1. The council commits who is pulling, and what kind of pull it is.
  2. Then the demand, the fits and the difficulty are declared, in the transcript.
  3. Then the roll.

The council chooses who goes before it is told how well they will do. If the scores came first, every pull would be handed to whoever scored highest and the choice would not be a choice. Committing blind is what makes sending the wrong person a real mistake and sending the right one a real read.

It also means the council pays for not knowing its own people - and the aptitude-log.md is how it learns them, one pull at a time, the same way the simulation does.

Declared at step 2, in the resolution record:

  1. What the lead demands - reading a ledger, reading a man, walking the ground, hearing a lie, knowing the law, knowing the dead.
  2. Each councillor's fit, as one clause and a score from -2 to +3, drawn from their strengths and weaknesses. A group takes the best fit, plus +1 if a second councillor also scores positive.
  3. The difficulty, 1-10.

Every such judgement is appended to ../infra/aptitude-log.md. The log is the consistency mechanism: once Keel is +3 on ledgers, he stays +3 on ledgers unless something in the fiction changed him, and a contradiction is visible on the page.

This keeps the characters open-ended. A thread that demands something nobody anticipated gets scored when it arrives, rather than being avoided because no stat covers it.

Difficulty

Declared with the fit, before the roll. The anchors are fixed, so that difficulty does not drift with how much the simulation wants a result.

DifficultyMeaning
Trivial2-3anyone with the time and the authority gets it. Rarely worth an action
Ordinary4-5a competent person gets it on most days. The right person gets it nearly always
Real6-7the standard for a first pull. The right person is favoured and is not safe
Hard8-9needs the right person and luck. Expect to fail this twice before clearing it
Closed10not reachable by asking. It wants a new angle, a favour, or an instrument the council does not yet hold

Default to 6. A thread's first pull sits at 6 or 7 unless there is a stated reason it is easier - and "the right councillor is on it" is not that reason. The fit is the reward for being suited; it is not also a discount on the difficulty. Scoring both is how a ruleset quietly stops having teeth.

Below 4 is for work that should probably not have cost an action at all.

Resolution

d10 + fit, against the difficulty.

Successtotal is at or above the difficulty
Margintotal minus difficulty
Bonusmargin of 3 or more

A d10 against a difficulty of 1-10 and a fit of -2 to +3 means the fit is worth about a third of the range. Competence tilts the odds and does not buy the result, which is the intended shape: Keel at +3 on a ledger fails a difficulty 7 roughly a quarter of the time, and a thing that is hard stays hard.

Difficulty is declared with the fit, before the roll, in the resolution record - transcripts/tick-NNN-resolution.md, not the scenes. The declarations are written before the fiction and the fiction may not contradict them, but no councillor ever says a number out loud. Split by the DM after tick 5.

What a pull yields

OutcomeYield
Failnothing. Full cost. Notice rolled anyway
Successthe minimum lead - the fact the thread was always going to give
Margin 3-5the minimum lead, plus one intel bonus
Margin 6+the minimum lead, plus either an intel bonus or a material one - the council chooses

The intel bonuses

  • A new thread, with its own substrate.
  • A substrate upgrade - hearsay becomes paper, and the decay rate drops to match. The strongest result available, and the rarest.
  • Decay arrested - the lead is written down, corroborated, nailed to a second source. The thread's decay rate halves, permanently. It still rests on what it rested on; it is simply better held. Weaker than an upgrade and available far more often, and it is the answer to a thread that is good and rotting.
  • The coverer's identity, if anyone has been covering.
  • The ground - a position on the map rather than a fact about a person. Where he went, where he is now, or the point he must pass. Added when the chase was built, because tracking yields a place and nothing on this list was a place. It is the only bonus that unlocks an interception, which is the cheapest way to take anybody - see chase.md.

This list is open, like the substrate and intrusiveness tables. A pull that plainly yields something not on it gets a new row rather than the nearest fit.

The material bonus - margin 5 and above only

They did not only find the lead. They found what was under it: goods worth confiscating, a debt somebody will pay to keep quiet, a person who now owes them. See windfalls.md for scale.

Two conditions, both hard.

  1. The fiction must supply it. A material bonus is a thing that was already there - a shed with more in it than the manifest says, a factor who needs an errand run. It is never conjured because the margin allowed it. If nothing material is on the ground, margin 5+ simply yields the intel bonus.
  2. It replaces the intel bonus. It is never added to it. Taking the money means giving up the lead they would have had at margin 3, and the thread is still 10 weaker for having been pulled.

That trade is the whole mechanic. A council that is losing can cash its own competence in to survive, and pays for it in the only currency this simulation is actually about.

The shape of the tiers

A competent pair, well paired to the demand - fit +4, being the best fit at +3 plus the +1 for a second positive councillor - against the default difficulty of 7:

NeedsChance
Successd10 >= 380%
Intel bonusd10 >= 650%
The choice unlocksd10 >= 830%

The success number is the design target, set by the DM: a well-matched pair succeeds four times in five. The material rate was a second target at one pull in five, and the DM lowered the threshold from 6 to 5 after tick 5, because in five ticks and fourteen scored checks it had never once fired. See ../infra/calibration-log.md.

That is a deliberate overshoot of the original 20%. The diagnosis was that the council spends single actions and a single cannot reach the tier - so the rate on paper was never the rate in play. Three in ten on paper is expected to land nearer the intended one in five in practice, and the number goes back up if it does not.

The fall-off is intentional. A +3 pair at difficulty 7 reaches the choice 20% of the time; a +2 pair 10%; a well-matched pair against a difficulty of 9 reaches it only on a 10, and nobody reaches it against 10. Hard things get done. They barely pay out.

Pulling does not consume a thread. It reduces its strength by a further 10, because the ground has been walked and the questions already asked.

The other side

Every adversary worth simulating has one action per tick, tracked sealed. One action, against the council's fifteen - the far side is not outmatched, because it knows what it is hiding and the council does not.

Its menu includes covering tracks, moving a person, planting a false lead, buying a favour, and advancing its own plan. Every cover is a week not spent on the plan, which is the real cost of hiding.

Covering tracks

A cover does three things:

  • an immediate strength hit, judged against what was covered
  • a permanent doubling of the decay rate, stacking on repeat
  • a burn, visible for one tick

The burn is the risk. If the council pulls a thread within one tick of it being covered, they get the normal yield and, guaranteed, a lead pointing at whoever did the covering. Covering the same thread twice running widens the burn window to two ticks.

An archived record is the exception: one cover takes it to zero outright, and a hole where a record should be is the loudest thing in this ruleset.

So covering is safe only when the far side is confident the council is looking elsewhere. That confidence is itself tracked, sealed - the far side can only act on what it believes about the council's attention, and it can be wrong.

The council cannot tell a covered thread from a neglected one by looking. Both read as fading. The difference only shows when they pull.

Where threads live

  • ../ledger/threads.md - what the council knows: name, substrate, freshness, whose lead it is, what it might give.
  • ../infra/sealed/threads.md - strength numbers, detection state, the far side's actions and beliefs, and what each thread actually leads to.
rules/investigations.md
rules

Leakage

What the council is owed, and what reaches the chest, are not the same number.

Legal leakage is already in the baseline. Everyone in the collection - weighers, tollkeepers, clerks - has been paid their due, and the figures in economy.md are post-payment. Nothing there needs modelling.

What needs modelling is the rest: theft, and refusal.

Who has hands on what

Collection runs through factions that already exist. No separate customs gauge - the people carrying the money are people the council can anger.

StreamCollectorShare
Passage tollThe Watch - gates, waggons50%
The harbour guard - quays, hulls50%
Bonded storageWarehousemen's guild - the yards70%
The Watch30%
Guild and market duesThe guilds, self-assessed and remitted100%

Each collector rolls separately, on its own share, against its own standing. The harbour guard is owned by the nobles and guild masters, so it inherits their temper along with their wages.

The skim

Clandestine. A clerk who should take a tenth takes two. The council is not told.

Chance per tickmax(0, -standing) x 2%, capped at 40%
Amount2% + 0.5% per point of negative standing, of that collector's share

A collector at 0 or above does not skim. There is no baseline dishonesty - being robbed by your own people is a consequence, not a constant.

This is why raising the toll is expensive twice. A fatter stream is a fatter thing to slice, so a rise hands the collectors a raise as well.

Finding it

The skim leaves a thread: working record, decay 2. Books do not reconcile and books do not forget.

  • An audit is a records query under authority - one action, standing with whoever's books they are. See investigations.md.
  • A skimming faction may spend actions covering, which drops it into the investigation mechanic as a far side of its own, burn window and all.
  • An audit that finds nothing still costs the action and still annoys the audited.

Once it is found

The council chooses, and each answer costs:

AnswerEffect
Prosecutethe money is recovered; standing with that collector falls hard, and with anyone who has ever taken a tenth
Negotiatea share is recovered, standing is unharmed, and the practice continues at a lower rate
Ignorenothing recovered. Other collectors notice, and their own chance rises

Ignoring is never free. A collection that learns theft is tolerated becomes a collection that steals.

Withholding

Open, declared, and known the moment it happens. This is what comes after a refused petition.

The ladder in petitions.md runs request, refusal, standing falls, petition. Withholding is the next rung: the faction keeps collecting and simply stops handing it over.

  • The council knows immediately, and knows exactly who.
  • The money is not stolen - it is owed. It goes to the Owed section of ../state/treasury.md and is recoverable by conceding, or by force.
  • It continues every tick until answered.

A strike by a collector is the most expensive thing a faction can do to this council without breaking a law, and it is available to any of them.

Robbery

External, visible, episodic - a tax-cart taken on the road. Not a standing problem: a roads-and-patrols problem, and the first thing that gives the Watch something to do besides be unpaid.

Built - see robbery.md, and delivered before tick 5 as the DM asked.

Two rolls. An attempt, keyed to darkness, bad roads and thin patrols, with nothing in it about wages or morale - robbers read the street, not the payroll. And an interception, keyed to the Watch's standing, because what the Watch thinks of the council decides only whether the men who find it wade in.

The shape of it

The skim is a mystery. The strike is a crisis. The robbery is an adventure. Three different kinds of week out of one leak.

rules/leakage.md
rules

Mass Combat

Armed men against armed men, normally with a councillor present to direct them. Too many people to simulate one at a time.

Not party combat - see party-combat.md for five individuals against a group. The two meet: see When the line breaks, below.

Cohesion is the number

Armies do not die to zero. They break and run.

If the quantity going down is men killed, a battle is a subtraction problem and morale has nowhere to live. The quantity that goes down here is whether the formation holds, which makes attrition, weariness and morale one number instead of three.

Cohesion, 0-100.

BandCohesion
Steady75-100will advance, will hold, will be commanded
Wavering50-74still a company, and everything is harder
Breaking25-49will not advance, and every lost exchange may rout it outright - see the hold check
Routed0-24it runs, and this is where men die

A company that holds loses few. A company that runs loses many, all at once. Most men who die in a battle die running away, and the ruleset should say so.

Casualties
Held the fieldroughly 5%
Withdrew in orderroughly 10%
Routed20-30%, and the enemy chooses how hard to pursue

Strategy and leadership are different things

This is the heart of it. Strategy is chosen before anyone is in sight. Leadership is what happens when the strategy turns out to be wrong.

Strategy - before the battle

A plan: what the company is trying to do, on what ground, against what the council believes it is facing. Declared before contact and checked once.

  • Costs 1 action, or 2 if the plan requires ground to be scouted, prepared or occupied first.
  • Scored on planning - reading ground, knowing what a body of men can actually do, anticipating the other side.
  • Score it against what is being planned against. A conventional enemy on known ground is one demand; something with no doctrine behind it - a thing out of a vault, a crew who do not fight the way soldiers fight - is a different one, and a doctrine-bound planner is worth less there. This is where slow to improvise bites, and it bites before the battle rather than during it.
  • On success it grants a standing modifier of +1 to +3 to every exchange, by the margin.
  • It is built on assumptions, and the assumptions are written down.

Adaptation - during the battle

When the battle departs from the plan - the enemy is not who they were believed to be, the ground gives way, a flank that was secure is not - the commander must adapt, and that is a separate check.

Adaptation is not the commander having a better idea. It is whether the company still does what it is told while the plan is visibly failing. Orders given in the open, to frightened men, by someone they will obey.

  • Scored on command presence - being obeyed under pressure. Steadiness, clarity, and whether the rankers know the voice.
  • Succeed: the strategy modifier holds, or is replaced by a new one.
  • Fail: the strategy modifier is lost for the rest of the battle. The orders are no longer reaching the line, and the company fights on whatever it has left.

Personal flexibility is not what is being scored. Brackwater is adaptable in any conditions and frightens people, and is a foreigner in a city that votes - she is a poor adapter here, because two hundred and fifty men do not know her voice and will not move on it. Garrow is cold under fire and trusted by rankers, and is excellent at it.

Garrow is simply good at war, at both halves of it, and the council's problem is not that he is uneven - it is that he is one man with three actions. A battle he attends is a week he is not spending on anything else, and a battle he cannot attend is commanded by somebody the Watch has no reason to obey. That is Garrow's version of Partridge's single point of failure, and the council has not yet had to look at it.

The exchange

Both sides roll:

d10 + leadership + equipment + ground + odds + strategy

The loser loses cohesion:

cohesion lost = 5 + (margin x 3)

A tie costs nobody anything. Two bodies of men have shouted at each other.

Equipment is a flat modifier and also decides what the company can impose - pikes demand a different fight from bows, the same way an enemy's background sets the demand in party combat.

The hold check - the cliff at Breaking

Cohesion is continuous. Breaking is not.

While a company is Breaking (25-49), every lost exchange forces a check before the cohesion is even applied:

d10 + command presence   against   4 + the margin of the loss
Holdthe company takes the normal cohesion loss and stays in the field
Failit routs outright, whatever the number says

Armies do not degrade linearly - they collapse, and a continuous number alone cannot produce that. This is the only place in the mechanic where the band matters more than the arithmetic.

It is also the moment the commander exists for. A +3 on command presence is the difference between a company that withdraws in order at 10% casualties and one that routs at 25%, decided in a single roll at the worst possible minute.

So is Garrow there? becomes the question at exactly the point it should be, instead of a flat modifier smeared across the whole battle.

Ground decides who sets the terms, exactly as preparation does in party combat. Taking it is usually the objective, and it can change hands mid-battle.

Headcount, and why it compounds

The odds

Modifier
Outnumbered 2:1 or worse-3
Outnumbered-1
Even0
Outnumbering+1
Outnumbering 2:1 or better+3

Casualties reduce strength, which worsens the odds, which produces more casualties. That is the attrition spiral, and it is one line of rules rather than a system.

Strength as a floor on what is possible

Absolute numbers decide what a company can be asked to do at all - hold a wall, cover a road, storm a place, picket a city of twenty-two thousand. A hundred men cannot do what two hundred and fifty can, whatever their cohesion.

Below half its established strength a company's ceiling drops by 20 (see below). A half-destroyed company does not come back to steady by resting.

Condition - the ceiling on recovery

Cohesion recovers between battles. It recovers toward a ceiling, and the ceiling is set by how the company is treated.

ceiling = 60 + standing        (maximum 100)
RecoveringPer tick
Resting in quarters, not deployed+10
In the field, not engaged+5
Engaged0

Modifiers to the ceiling:

Below half established strength-20
Wages in arrearsalready in the standing

The standing is the morale. No new gauge: a company's standing in ../ledger/factions.md is already the sum of a reasoned log of how the council has treated it, which is exactly what morale is.

What that means for Needleford today

The Watch stands at -19. Its ceiling is 41 - breaking.

The council cannot presently field the Watch against anybody. However long they rest, however well led, they cap out at a band where they will not advance and one bad exchange routs them. Nothing fixes that except paying them and being worth following.

Nobody designed that. It is eight weeks of arrears and one audit, read back as a number.

When the line breaks

If the company routs, or the line is broken around a councillor, it becomes party combat. Same beats, same acts, same three-level cap, with the opposition set by whatever got through.

A councillor who went to direct a battle is now in one.

Strength, pay and recruiting

Tracked live in ../ledger/the-watch.md.

The council sets the Watch's size and its wage, and bankrolls both. It may build whatever army it can afford. The payroll is headcount times wage, every tick, and it is the largest line in the cost base.

Establishment - the council's number

The council sets the establishment: how many men the Watch is supposed to have. It may raise it or lower it at will, and it pays for every man on the roll, every week, for as long as he is on it.

Recruiting fills the gap between present strength and establishment, and nothing else. A Watch at full strength recruits nobody and costs nothing extra. Recruiting only happens after losses, or after the council decides it wants a bigger army and has worked out how to pay for one.

Recruiting

Governed by time. Standing sets the rate - a city recruits for a council it respects, and grudgingly for one it does not.

Watch standingMen per tick
-50 or below0
-401
-301
-202
-103
05
+107
+2010
+3015
+4021
+50 or above30

Interpolate between rows. A disliked council still recruits - slowly, and from a worse pool - and only an actively hated one cannot.

The wage as a lever

Each extra gold per week on the wage counts as +5 standing, for recruiting only. Paying 12G instead of 10G recruits like a council ten points better liked. Paying less counts against, by the same arithmetic.

It does not buy goodwill, cohesion or willingness. It buys men at the gate, and it costs the full amount every week for every man already on the roll - which is the trap. Raising the wage to recruit fifty men means raising it for the two hundred and fifty you already have.

Desertion

Not modelled yet. Parked by the DM. Men do not currently leave the Watch over unpaid wages, which is generous and known to be generous.

Dials

Provisional, and the first things to turn. Logged in ../infra/calibration-log.md as they are.

  • 60 + standing for the ceiling. The single most consequential number here - it decides how good a council has to be to have an army.
  • 5 + margin x 3 for cohesion loss, which sets how many exchanges a battle runs.
  • The odds table, and whether 2:1 should be worth more than 3.
  • Rout casualties at 20-30%.
  • The recruiting table, which has never been tested because the Watch has never been anything but 250, and the +5 standing per gold of wage.
  • 4 + margin for the hold check at Breaking - the number that decides how survivable the bottom band is.

Open

  • One company or several. Written for one. Several would give real manoeuvre and a great deal more bookkeeping; added only if a battle wants it.
  • Sieges. A siege is not a sequence of exchanges and should not be forced into one.
rules/mass-combat.md
rules

Party Combat

Five individuals, or a subset of them plus any named NPCs with them, against a group. A dungeon. An ambush laid. An ambush walked into.

Not the rule for armed men against armed men - see mass-combat.md. Not the rule for a pursuit - see chase.md, which ends in this file whenever the quarry spends the far side's action turning and fighting.

The health ladder

From character-sheets.md, and it applies to every named character on either side:

healthy - injured - bloodied - dying - dead

A single fight may take a given character down at most three levels.

  • A healthy councillor cannot be killed in one fight. Healthy plus three is dying, not dead.
  • A councillor who walks in already hurt can be. Injured plus three is dead.

Going in wounded is the danger. The second raid in a fortnight is the one that kills somebody, and the council will have to choose between tempo and bodies knowing it.

The beat

A fight is a run of beats. Both sides roll every beat and the rolls are compared.

party:  d10 + the lead's fit        against
enemy:  d10 + their quality
Party wins the beatone hit on the enemy
Enemy wins the beatone hit on the party - a wound, one level
A tienothing. Two people have swung at each other and both are still standing

Who gets hit

Whoever won the beat chooses the target - and the choice must be narratively sensible.

A brute cannot reach Thackeray while Garrow is between them. He goes through Garrow or he goes nowhere. Equally, the party cannot put a blade in the enemy mage at the back merely because he is the one they would like to kill.

So shielding is not nominated, it is positional. Standing in front of somebody is a fact about where people are, not a declaration made at the moment of the wound - and it holds until somebody changes it.

Which makes position the thing worth fighting over. Putting a man on the ground is not only a hit; it opens a lane that was closed. The beat that knocks the front rank down is the beat that lets the next one reach the mage.

There is no difficulty number in party combat. The enemy is scored the same way a councillor is - -2 to +3 against what the beat demands, read off what they are. A trained housebreaker working by stealth is +3 when the beat demands stealth and poor when somebody sets the room on fire.

How a fight ends

  • The enemy breaks after N hits, where N is their headcount adjusted for quality. This is the same headcount-as-health the far side already runs on.
  • The party breaks when it decides to, or when nobody is left who can absorb a wound without being killed by it.

A fight the council is winning ends early. A fight it is losing ends when it admits it. Neither side has a hit-point sheet - one side has bodies and the other has a health ladder, and both were already written down.

The three-level cap forces damage to spread

Garrow can stand in front until he is dying. Then somebody else has to bleed. A long fight cannot be absorbed by one willing body, and when nobody is left who can take a hit without dying of it, the party is out of road and must break off.

Order of play

Commit-first, as everywhere else.

  1. The party commits - who leads this beat, who absorbs a loss, whether an act is being spent.
  2. Then the demand, the lead's fit and the enemy's quality are declared.
  3. Then both rolls.

Acts - one each, for the whole fight

Every named character gets exactly one act per fight. Councillors, and named NPCs on either side. Unnamed bodies are headcount and nothing else.

The skill is timing, not inventory. Thackeray's ward is precious because she cannot do it twice, and holding it for the beat that needs it is the entire decision.

What an act can do - the four dials

A fight has exactly four parameters, and an act turns one of them. There is no spell list. What a character may reach is read off their sheet, the same way a fit is.

DialWhat it looks like
Our rollBrackwater's alchemy, a position taken, a weapon prepared
Their rollsmoke, a ward against enemy magic, blinding, fouling the ground
HitsGarrow putting men down - a hit scored directly, without a beat
Positionopening a lane, closing one, getting between two people, forcing a line to break. Decides who can be hit at all
The demand, or their preparationfiring the fortification they dug into; dragging a stealth fight into the open where stealth is worth nothing

Hits and position overlap on purpose. Putting a man down removes a body and opens the ground he was standing on, which is why knocking people over is worth an act and not merely a flourish.

Healing mid-fight is our roll or hits depending on the fiction - a level restored is a level the party can still absorb, and absorbable levels are the party's real stamina.

Ongoing effects, and negating them

An act may leave something standing: smoke, a ward, fouled ground, a fire.

  • An ongoing effect lasts at least one full beat. It cannot be pre-empted.
  • After that it may be negated by the other side spending an act - magic wind clears a smokescreen, a dispel takes down a forcefield.
  • Negating costs the enemy their act, which is the trade. A ward that forces a trained enemy to spend his one act dispelling it has already paid for itself.

This is why the enemy having acts matters. Every named enemy can answer, and every answer is a thing they are not doing to the party instead.

Preparation - who chose the ground

One axis, three rungs. The defender holds the ground unless the attacker takes it from them.

Dug inknew it was coming and chose the ground. Sets what every beat demands, and +1 to their beat rolls
Readyknew, with no time to arrange. A straight fight
Surprisedcaught cold. Loses the first beat with no roll - somebody is hurt before anyone has decided anything - and the other side sets the demand throughout

This is already being tracked

Preparation is the detection state. Every notice roll in investigations.md has been quietly buying the far side a rung on this ladder since tick 1, and the council has never been told.

It cuts both ways. Preparation is purchasable: surveillance, a scouting pull, a records query that gives the ground before anyone walks it. Investigation is no longer only the mystery half of the game - it is what decides whether a fight is survivable.

Breaking off

Available between any two beats.

  • The party takes no further beats.
  • One member, chosen at random, takes a hit that cannot pass dying. Retreat is not free and it is not fatal.
  • They lose the objective, and the far side learns what they came for, which is often the more expensive half.

Death

A councillor can die. It is on the table and it is not softened.

A councillor's death is a guaranteed halt - unconditional, exempt from the halt budget, and already an irreversibility-4 trip-wire under simulation.md.

There is no one to replace them. Losing one is a catastrophe, not a staffing problem.

Recovery

Dying

  • Dying costs all three actions. A dying councillor does nothing.
